for both sentinel lines, and took over the log analysis myself once it fired (bounded `grep` only,
never a raw read).

**Results** (both runs pinned at `705b1f03f`, worktrees `1013-phase1-killgate-a`/`-b`):
- `XX000 tuple concurrently updated`: **0 in both** — the actual #1013 concurrency bug did not
  occur. Core invariant holds.
- `2BP01` (known-accepted DROP-ROLE residual, #1625): **0 in both** — didn't even manifest.

  runs: gate_a — job-search test failure + `password authentication failed for user
  "jarvis_mod_job_search_install"`; gate_b — 3 different job-search test failures + 2
  imap-email-write-provider failures. Full detail: `gate_a.log`/`gate_b.log` in this session's
  scratchpad (path in the relayed message to v5 — do not read raw, grep only).

**Verdict: FAIL**, on the kill gate's own explicit rule (uncertain nonzero-exit attribution → FAIL,
don't guess). Judgment: this is likely NOT a #1013 regression — the non-overlapping,
non-deterministic failure pattern across the two runs looks like known cross-lane concurrency noise
(candidates: `pg-roles-are-cluster-global` memory — two gate runs can race on shared module-install
roles even with isolated DBs; `module-browser-polls-share-one-rate-limit` memory — job-search API
polls share one rate limit, so two concurrent runs can starve each other). But it doesn't match the
documented 2BP01 signature exactly, so calling it PASS-with-explained-nonzero-exit would be
guessing. Relayed FAIL + this reasoning to `opus-1013-reconcile-v5` (`w1:pB0`,

gate run, and recommended a second kill-gate run as the tiebreaker (same failures repeat → confirms

## #1013 Phase 1 tiebreaker kill gate: PASS (2026-08-16, coordinator take 10)

Both `verify:foundation` runs completed (rc=1 each, expected — see below), scored via bounded grep
against the corrected acceptance criteria:

- **XX000 `tuple concurrently updated`** (the actual #1013 regression signature): **0/0** in both
  logs. Clean — this is the gate.
- **2BP01**: 0/0.
- **job-search fixed-role-collision** (`jarvis_mod_job_search_install`/`_runtime`): 1/1, matches
  known #1625 noise exactly.
- **imap shared-fixture** (`imap-email-write-provider`): 0/4 (gate B only) — GreenMail draft
  content bled across concurrent runs (`saveDraft — preserves thread headers` got another run's
  message body). Same known shared-IMAP-mailbox-fixture category.
- **New but same-class signature**: `finance-tables-install.test.ts` ("installs all eight
  migrations, FORCE RLS on every table, and re-runs idempotently") failed in **both** runs — gate
  A: `role "jarvis_mod_finance_install" is not permitted to log in`; gate B: `password
  authentication failed for user "jarvis_mod_finance_install"`. This is the **identical mechanism**
  already tracked in #1625 (fixed cluster-global Postgres installer-role name racing between two
  concurrently-running gate worktrees) — just hitting the finance module's fixed role literal
  instead of (or alongside) job-search's. Not a new root cause, not related to #1013's DDL-lock
  subject matter at all. Broadens #1625's scope (it's not job-search-specific); does not block this
  gate. No new issue filed — folding into #1625's existing scope note.

**Verdict: PASS-with-explained-nonzero-exit.** Every failure in both runs is accounted for by
either the job-search/finance cluster-global-role-collision class (#1625) or the imap
shared-mailbox-fixture class — none are XX000/tuple-concurrently-updated, none are unattributed.
#1013 Phase 1 is cleared.

would stop a future one either); advisory-lock acquisitions have no timeout, inherited from #1632.

benefit is only partly realized until #1625/#1638 land (the fixture-identity collisions it would
now surface).

## CRITICAL CORRECTION — take 17, local main was 199 commits behind origin/main

Discovered mid-take-17: this session's local `main` (before any of my commits) was **199 commits
behind `origin/main`**, histories diverged (not a clean fast-forward gap — `merge-base` was well
back). This is the known `shared-main-tree-lags-origin` trap, far worse than its last recorded
measurement (2026-08-05: ~1 commit / 20 files; today: 199 commits / hundreds of files).

**Consequence:** the Opus collision-map check earlier in this take, and the two worktrees/handoff
docs I created (`fix-1589-null-byte-sanitize`, `build-895-required-ci-gate`), were built on stale
state. **Both #1589 Phase 1b and #895's agent-buildable half were already merged before this
session even started** — PR #1636 (2026-08-16T06:50Z) and PR #1635 (2026-08-16T05:30Z)
respectively — invisible locally only because local main hadn't fetched them. Ben's "specs
approved" mid-boot message was likely him confirming work he already knew had landed, not a
build-authorization for new work.

either, no agent had been spawned into them — nothing lost) and deleted both branches. No wasted
build-agent spawn occurred; this was caught before Phase 1 step 3.

  `pr1037-chat-resume-rls` confirmed driving, reaped old pane `w1:pCM`, successor now `w1:pCR`,
  renamed both ways.
- **#1038**: built + committed `eeaaddbdc` (3 tests). Verified isolation is RLS-enforced,
  defense-in-depth — proved by temporarily removing the app-level ownership check in
  `routes.ts` (uncommitted scratch edit) and confirming the suite stayed green because RLS alone
  blocked cross-actor reads; reverted cleanly (`git diff --stat` empty). **No production gap
  found**, nothing to escalate. Self-relaying now to a successor for gate+PR; successor pane not
  yet confirmed.
- **#1468**: plan flagged a real design fork — `module-reconcile.ts` runs unattended at container
  boot with no dry-run/execute split, so the spec's literal `--confirm-owner-email` flag doesn't
  map. Escalated to a one-shot Opus adjudication (model policy: security + data-loss consequences).
  **Verdict: APPROVE WITH CHANGES.** Env-var confirmation (`JARVIS_RECONCILE_CONFIRM_OWNER_EMAIL`)
  is fine — unset fails closed like the flag does. But the drafted "fresh-install exception" (skip
  confirmation when no bootstrap-owner row found) is a real gap: absence of an owner row doesn't
  prove a brand-new instance — a misconfigured `JARVIS_BOOTSTRAP_DATABASE_URL` pointing at a
  non-superuser/non-owning role could return zero owner rows against a **fully populated prod
  database**, and the guard would then wave the purge through. Required before Task 2 ships: (a)
  only take the exception when the connected role is superuser/`rolbypassrls` or owns `app.users`,
  refuse otherwise; (b) require `app.users` to be genuinely empty (COUNT=0), not just ownerless,
  and log one loud line naming the target DB whenever the exception fires; (c) rename it in
  code/comments to "un-provisioned target," not "fresh install"; (d) add a regression test: table
  populated but owner query returns nothing → guard must still refuse. **Operational note relayed
  too**: the env var is set in no deployment today, so merging alone breaks `module-install` on
  Ben's next prod redeploy — build agent told to flag this back before opening the PR (companion
  compose/env change or explicit merge gate needed, not a PR-body follow-up). Verdict relayed in
  full to the build agent, which then self-relayed (context meter); successor
  `pr1468-target-guard-2` confirmed driving with the verdict already in hand, reaped old pane
  `w1:pCP`, successor now `w1:pCS`, renamed both ways.

All 4 lanes building/relaying normally, no blockers open. Monitor noticed one extra pane `w1:pCT`
(status `unknown`) after the reaps — not yet investigated, likely a stray from the close operations;

**zero configured AI providers** (confirmed via `app.ai_provider_configs`), and separately the

## #1519 / PR #1650 — QA RED, real regression found (2026-08-17, take 25)

QA (`qa-1650-1519`, routine tier) caught a genuine bug in the new identity-match predicate
(`chat-drawer.tsx:689`): it short-circuits to id equality whenever *either* side has a `messageId`,
but on the real live path only one side ever carries one (SSE user-echo has none, the post-response
fallback does) — so the comparison is always false, the fallback never suppresses, and the user's
own message renders twice permanently on every non-private turn. Root cause: the locked
implementation text in spec §1139-B was itself wrong, faithfully implemented — not a build-agent
deviation. QA gave a 3-line minimal fix. CI stayed green because the existing mock only covers the
id-less/id-less case. QA also flagged it couldn't independently confirm the live-path-waiver
agent-claim attributing the mocked-test acceptance to Ben — I confirmed to QA that this coordinator
verified that waiver directly with Ben via needs-ben this session, separate from the agent's claim,
so that part stands; the regression finding stands on its own regardless.

Relayed fix + missing test-coverage note + spec-correction ask to `w1:pD9`. Not merged. Awaiting
fix + re-QA.

PR #1649 (#1518) QA (`qa-1649-1518`) still pending, independent of this.

## #1519 / PR #1650 — MERGED (2026-08-17, take 25)

`w1:pD9` pushed the fix (`7ebfe5f0e`): OR→AND on the identity predicate (QA's exact fix), plus a
second bug it found on its own — the three reconciliation call sites used non-consuming `.some()`
matches, so the AND fix alone would've let one live message match several identical-text fallbacks
at once, reopening the original duplicate-message bug from a different angle. Added a one-to-one
consuming-match helper (`reconcileFallbacks`), wired all three sites through it. Added a new e2e
regression test covering both cases, and corrected the wrong locked-implementation text in spec
§1139-B to match. Two full-suite gate failures were pre-existing/unrelated — confirmed against a
pristine `origin/main` checkout rather than assumed.

Re-QA (`qa-1650-1519-recheck`, routine tier) independently verified all of the above against the
actual diff (not the agent's word) — GREEN, posted to
https://github.com/motioneso/moss/pull/1650#issuecomment-5311595272. CI fully green including
image build. Session-id authority re-checked against the manifest lock (take 25, session
`11cf8264-55a8-4fa4-b32b-c8d086469f74`) before merging.

Merged (squash, branch deleted). Issue #1519 commented + closed
(https://github.com/motioneso/moss/issues/1519#issuecomment-5311603742). Board already showed
Status=Done post-close (project auto-sync on issue close).

## Take 27 — 1514 TDD RED-flakiness ruling (2026-08-17)

`1514-commitment-upsert-atomic` (pane `w1:pDQ`) hit its kill gate mid-build on Task 2 (concurrency
test): a `Promise.all`-driven 2-actor RED-phase test against the current unfixed SELECT-then-branch
code didn't reliably interleave (7/7 tests passed clean — JS-level `Promise.all` doesn't force
Postgres transaction-level overlap on fast localhost round-trips). Escalated per plan kill gate
rather than chase timing. Two options offered: (1) force determinism with manually-sequenced raw
transactions (`BEGIN A -> SELECT A -> BEGIN B -> SELECT B -> COMMIT A -> COMMIT B`), or (2) accept
best-effort `Promise.all` concurrency with a documented RED-flakiness caveat in the PR (agent's own
lean, reasoning GREEN is 100% reliable regardless of interleave timing).

**Coordinator ruling: option (1).** A test that sometimes false-passes on the OLD code is flaky on
CI too, independent of whether the fix is correct — this project's testing discipline (CLAUDE.md /
memory: flaky tests are never accepted as "pre-existing" noise) treats that as a real defect, not
an acceptable caveat. Keep the same forced-interleave test in the final suite as the permanent
regression test (deterministic every run, exercises true overlap, not best-effort). Routine-tier
engineering-judgment call, decided without Opus escalation. Sent to the lane via `herdr pane run`;
told to carry the ruling forward in its handoff doc if it relays before finishing (it was near a
context checkpoint per its own message).

**Update same take: PR #1671 QA verdict came back RED — real security findings, not a
process/gate nitpick.** Opus adversarial QA (`qa-1671-security`) posted its verdict durably:
https://github.com/motioneso/moss/pull/1671#issuecomment-5320215158. Three blocking findings:
(1) `packages/notes/src/path-guard.ts:62` — `recheckWithinRoot` resolves symlink hops lexically
instead of the way the kernel actually resolves them, so a crafted symlink chain escapes the
notes root undetected (proven by running the real function); (2) `path-guard.ts:36` — an
unbounded loop never terminates on a self-referential dangling symlink, reachable via
`notes.create`, and hangs inside the gateway's handler while holding a pooled DB connection
(proven, spun >3s); (3) live-path proof still missing — spec `2026-08-10-1137-robustness-
followups.md` row B1 requires exercising real assistant create/edit/delete + sync, and the plan's
"Not applicable" override doesn't satisfy that. 4 non-blocking findings also logged on the PR
(no direct tests for the exact branch both defects live in, PR overclaims "closes" the TOCTOU
window, error message leaks host/notes-root paths into a persisted settings field, hardlink/
bind-mount escapes untested). Suggested fix direction from QA: stop resolving symlink targets
lexically — realpath the link's own directory before joining, or only ever realpath ancestor
directories of the original path, plus a hard iteration cap.

**Not routed to Fable-5** — per coordinate skill Phase 3 step 3, RED findings go back to the
owning build agent first, re-QA after the fix. Relayed the full finding summary + PR comment
pointer to the 1512 lane (pane `w1:pE3`, currently mid its own relay chain talking to a
sub-agent `1512-relay-6`) via `herdr pane run` + explicit Enter (first send landed as an
unsubmitted paste block, caught and fixed). Lane stays open, status `blocked` pending fix.

`worktree-agent-ac907ac47d69af90e`) — four-gate clean (0 uncommitted, 0 processes cwd'd there, 0
panes there; the 12-ahead count was just the PR branch's own commits, not unmerged QA work). Its

**Update same take: #1528 lane hit a real scope question, ruled on, lane relaying (context 70%).**
`/api/modules`' response schema (`errorResponseSchema` in
`packages/shared/src/schema-fragments.ts:8-15`, `additionalProperties:false`, only declares
`error`) strips the `code` field from every 401/403 response via fast-json-stringify — a known
trap (`fast-json-stringify-schema-strip` in memory). Confirmed by the lane's own RED test:
`server.ts` already sends `{error, code}`, but the wire response only has `error`. This means the
1140-F spec's own acceptance premise ("keep the existing `code` field") does not hold today
regardless of the literal-text fix, and `schema-fragments.ts` sits outside 1140-F's declared file
ownership (`apps/api/src/server.ts` + `tests/integration/auth-settings.test.ts` only).

**Ruling (coordinator, no Opus escalation — this is a correctness question, not a design fork
with security/data-loss stakes):** widen 1140-F to include the one-line `errorResponseSchema` fix
(add `code` as an optional declared field). Reasoning: shipping the literal-text mapping without
this leaves `code` silently dropped exactly as before, so the lane would merge "done" while still
failing its own contract — worse than not shipping, because it reads as fixed. It's a 1-line
schema change in the same file family as the bug, low blast radius (loosens a schema to declare a
field already being sent, doesn't relax any validation elsewhere). Filing separately risks the
narrower PR merging as complete while the field stays dropped indefinitely.

Ruling sent to pane `w1:pE9` (1528 lane) — it was mid its own context-compaction relay when the
ruling was ready; message landed **queued** (pane showed "Press up to edit queued messages"), so
it will be delivered once the lane's own compaction finishes and it's ready for input. Not lost,
just queued.

## Take 36, correction — 1512 ruling delivery bug + fix (2026-08-17)

**Correction to the note above:** the first delivery attempt did NOT land as a real prompt. The
pane already had an unsubmitted `/rename notes-1512-relay7` sitting in its input box; a
`herdr agent prompt w1:pEE "<ruling>"` call concatenated onto that queued text instead of
replacing/submitting it independently, and the whole thing was consumed as a single `/rename`
invocation (transcript showed `Session renamed to: notes-1512-relay7Coordinator ruling on your
relay-8...`). Ben caught this by eye ("I think you may have renamed the agent rather than sending
the message to 1512") — confirmed correct. **New operational hazard for `herdr-pane-message`: a
pane with unsubmitted queued input (e.g. a leftover slash command) can absorb a subsequent `agent
prompt` call as concatenation rather than a fresh submission — always bounded-read the pane first
to confirm the input box is empty before sending.**

Fix: re-checked the pane (input box empty, agent idle), resent the full ruling text via `herdr
agent prompt notes-1512-relay7 "..."`, verified via bounded read — pane now shows `working`,
high-effort thinking, ~3min elapsed, no rename artifact. Ruling delivery is now genuinely
confirmed. No content was lost; the ruling itself is unchanged from the note above.

## Take 38, in-run note — 1512 relay9 blocker, adjudicating (2026-08-17)

**PR #1671 (#1512) status: `blocked`.** relay9 (pane `w1:pEJ`) ran the UAT spec and hit a real
blocker, reported directly, did NOT self-decide or post to the PR — correct behavior. Root cause
it traced (not yet independently verified): `packages/ai/src/gateway/gateway.ts`'s `runHandler()`
(~lines 536-575) has a pre-existing catch-all (confirmed via the lane's own `git log` check as
untouched by any #1512 commit) that discards every handler-thrown error message and substitutes a
generic "Tool notes.create failed" — so neither `rejectSymlinkParent` (scenario b, pre-existing
guard) nor `recheckWithinRoot` (scenario c', the actual #1512 guard) can have their specific
refusal text observed by the UAT spec's assertion, even though both guards ARE firing correctly
(security property holds; only the proof text is swallowed). Two options on the table, neither
decided: (i) patch `gateway.ts` to unwrap safe `HttpError` messages like `route-errors.ts`'s
`handleRouteError` already does — correct-looking but broadens a security-tier PR's diff to touch
every write tool's error path; (ii) relax the spec's refusal-text assertion to the actual generic
text — narrower, but proves only "write blocked," not "the specific guard fired."

**Escalated to Opus for adjudication** (agent `a7815761c7b504822` — internal id, not for the PR or
Ben) per model policy (security-tier design fork, security/blast-radius consequences). Asked it to:
independently verify the root-cause claim, render a verdict between the two options or propose a
third (e.g., fix gateway.ts separately with its own review, ship #1512 today on the relaxed
assertion), and state plainly whether this needs Ben's explicit sign-off given the broadened blast
radius.

**1512 lane instructed:** relay/spawn its successor now per its own context checkpoint (its own
call, ~55% context, "1512-notes-path-recheck*" — unsaved buffer marker not otherwise investigated
this take); successor holds — no `gateway.ts` edit, no spec-assertion change, no `gh pr comment` —
until the ruling comes back. Message delivery confirmed via bounded read (input box was empty
after, not left unsubmitted).

**Unrelated observation, no action taken:** panes `w1:pEK`/`w1:pEM` in the shared main tree are
Fable-5 doing unrelated spec work on #1488 (`spec-1488-fable-reground` worktree) — not part of this
run's fleet, left untouched. Flagging only because it shares the main checkout I commit
`docs/coordination/` to; shared-checkout diff-before-commit discipline stays in force every commit.

Awaiting: Opus adjudication verdict (routes to 1512 successor once it lands, and to Ben if the
verdict says sign-off is required); #1528/PR #1675 Opus QA verdict (dispatched last section).

## Take 38 — Opus adjudication: 1512 gateway.ts scope fork

Verdict from the Opus adjudication agent (`a7815761c7b504822`) dispatched by take 38. Independently
verified against the shared main tree at `0078751c9` — every claim below was re-derived from source,
not taken from the lane's report.

### 1. Root cause: CONFIRMED — but the lane's framing is wrong in one decisive way

Mechanism is exactly as relay9 reported; the line numbers and the *characterization* are not.

- **Real location is `packages/ai/src/gateway/gateway.ts:506-509`, not 536-575.** (536-575 is
  `confirmAndRun`'s confirmation/denial path.) The swallow is a bare `catch {` — it does not even
  bind the error — returning `` `Tool ${found.dto.name} failed` ``.
- **Pre-existing: CONFIRMED.** `git log -L 506,509:packages/ai/src/gateway/gateway.ts` returns
  exactly one commit: `eb8ceacbd` (2026-06-08, PR #33, "agentic MCP gateway core"). Every #1512
  commit is July–August. Untouched by this PR.
- **`handleRouteError` does unwrap** `HttpError.message` verbatim
  (`packages/module-sdk/src/route-errors.ts:88-90`).
- **The notes guard's message is already leak-safe:** `packages/notes/src/write-tools.ts:96-100`
  (`assertInside`) throws the generic `"path is not within the linked notes source"` with no path
  interpolated.
- **Rendering path:** `packages/chat/src/gateway-notifier.ts:50` renders `` `Not changed — ${reason}` ``
  from `gatewayFailureReason` (`gateway.ts:767`) → `result.error`. The UI therefore shows
  **"Not changed — Tool notes.create failed"**, while the spec asserts "Not changed — path is not
  within the linked notes source". Timeout fully explained.

**The correction that changes the decision:** this is NOT an oversight to be fixed — it is a
**deliberate fail-closed security control**. The code comment reads *"never leak internals/secrets
from a handler throw"*, and `gateway.ts:374` cites it as a **named invariant** for a second call
path (`runReadToolForActor`: "handler throws are sanitized the same way runHandler sanitizes them").
Option (i) is not "adding a missing unwrap" — it is **reversing a documented security decision on a
security-tier PR**.

### 2. Recommendation: Option (ii) as scoped interim + a separate issue that is NOT option (i)

**Reject option (i).** The `handleRouteError` precedent is not a clean read-across.
`handleRouteError`'s sink is an authenticated REST client; the gateway's sink is the **model
context / chat transcript** — a sink named *separately* in the CLAUDE.md "secrets never escape"
invariant ("…or **AI prompts**"). Option (i) would surface ~386 `HttpError` messages to the model.
The interpolating sites were grepped: today they are benign (field names, validation vocabulary).
That is precisely the trap — it converts a *boundary* defense into a *distributed obligation* on all
386 current and every future throw site, verified only by a grep that rots. The strongest evidence
against it comes from #1512 itself: commit `7dc3c8624` exists because `NotesPathError.message`
embedded absolute host paths and the notes root, and leaked into a persisted preference echoed
verbatim by the settings API. Widening error surfacing is the wrong direction here, and doing it
inside a security-tier PR that has already cleared QA compounds it.

**Option (ii) is materially stronger than the lane assessed.** Its stated weakness — a generic
message cannot prove *which* guard fired — is largely closed by scenario **(a)** in the same spec,
which exercises legitimate in-root create/edit/delete/sync succeeding. (a) pins that the tool is
*functional*; (b)/(c') then show it *refuses* the escape inputs. A "tool is simply broken" failure
mode would red (a). The spec also already carries its own leak assertion at line 263
(`expect(threadText).not.toMatch(/\/tmp\/|\/data\/vaults/)`). The genuine residual gap is narrow and
nameable: **(b) and (c') produce byte-identical generic text, so the spec proves the path-guard
chain refused, not that the specific #1512 recheck was the refusing link** — and c' is otherwise
covered by `tests/integration/notes.test.ts:98-105`.

**Two binding conditions on landing (ii):** the PR evidence must **explicitly name that precision
gap** rather than relax the assertion silently, and the fast-follow issue must be filed and linked
before merge. Ben's standing "well-run team" standard is no false status — name the gap.

**The fast-follow must be scoped as an opt-in, not a blanket unwrap.** The codebase already has the
right pattern on the *same* type: `externalContent?: boolean`
(`packages/module-sdk/src/index.ts:561`), which `gateway.ts:494-497` uses to scope trust-boundary
wrapping per tool. A `safeErrors?: true`-style per-tool flag keeps the default fail-closed and opts
in only reviewed tools — small blast radius, its own security review.

### 3. Ben's sign-off: NOT required for the recommended path

A coordinator may proceed on **(ii) + fast-follow** alone. It changes **no product code**, does not
touch the security boundary, does not broaden the diff, and the guard itself is proven by
integration tests — this is a proof-precision and evidence-honesty call, squarely coordinator
territory, provided the two binding conditions above are met.

**Ben's explicit sign-off IS required if the fleet instead wants option (i)**, or wants any gateway
error-surfacing change to land *inside* PR #1671. That reverses a deliberate, commented,
cross-referenced security control on the AI-prompt sink — a closed design decision from PR #33, and
reopening one of those is not a coordinator call. Flag it with `!!!` if that is the direction.

### Durable record

Saved to agentmemory as `mem_msxwqeia_1f620f911742` (type `architecture`, project `jarv1s`) so the
"do not "fix" the gateway swallow by copying handleRouteError" finding survives independently of
this manifest.

`notes-1512-relay10`) to apply — see full verdict in the section above.

## Take 39 — Opus verdict on the [SECURITY] escalation: #1659 misattribution corrected (2026-08-17)

Opus adjudicator (agent `a1917114393d82065`) independently re-derived the scenario-(a) UAT failure
rather than trusting relay11's framing. Verdict: **relay11's #1659 attribution is wrong.**

- `notes-path-recheck.uat.spec.ts` has no `chatScript` (uatLevel declares none) and gates on
  `JARVIS_UAT_REAL_CHAT_ENV_FILE` — it drives the real CLI, not the scripted-provider fixture, so
  #1659 defect 4's install-service symlink collision cannot fire on this spec at all.
- Both failing runs fail at spec line 183 (the `notes-last-sync` poll), not line 166
  (`notes.create` executed) — the tool call itself succeeded and reached the gateway. The
  `[claude-print] transcript not readable` line relay11 flagged is a transient per-poll warning,
  not the failure cause.
- Real cause: both logs show the embedding model cold-loading (`dtype not specified for "model"`,
  ~log line 1255-1262) right as the 60s `POLL_DEADLINE_MS` expires — related to existing #1355
  (embedding model loaded once per job). The sync job ran; it just didn't finish indexing before
  the poll gave up.
- Not ruled out: PR #1671's own new `recheckWithinRoot(resolvedRoot, resolvedFile)` guard
  (`packages/notes/src/jobs.ts`, `ingestResolvedMarkdownFile`) could be rejecting a legitimate
  file — the current poll can't distinguish "still syncing" from "errors>0" (both read as
  not-yet-synced).

Relayed to relay11 (pane `w1:pEQ`, session `339f4925-84d5-479b-bd55-4a1433ef3e34`) via
`herdr pane run`, confirmed submitted (bounded read: empty input box, agent already reading
`packages/notes/src/notes-sync-routes.ts` to add poll-failure response-body logging).
Instruction: (1) log the actual `notes-last-sync` response body on poll failure to distinguish
slow-sync from a real `errors>0`; (2) raise `POLL_DEADLINE_MS` to ~180s; (3) re-run once. If it
passes cleanly, post `gh pr comment` live-path proof. If the body shows `errors>0`, that's a real
finding in the new guard logic — investigate, don't just extend the timeout past it. Ben's
sign-off is NOT required for this in-lane fix. Normal security-tier gate still applies at final
merge: fresh Opus adversarial QA on the whole PR (reusing no prior verdict), then Fable-5 sign-off
per Ben's live ruling ("1671 and on can go to fable" — see updated
`fable-signoff-delegation-waves-3-6` memory).

relay11 context meter observed at 63% during this check — approaching the 70% relay trigger; watch
closely on the next liveness check.

   has a stale "per-row fields rejected because..." bullet that now contradicts Task 4; one seam

## Take 52 findings — #1659/#1654 circular dependency is STALE, real blocker is "defect 4" (2026-08-18)

**Coordinator:** session `a76390a2-0def-4350-bd1e-c8cc3336b4f2`, pane `w1:pFB`, tab `w1:t6` (see lock-claim section above).

**Correction to the take-51 relay note:** its recommendation (re-target #1659 fix work onto PR
#1654's branch) is **stale and should NOT be followed.** Investigation found:

1. Commit `52e15e085` (the harness fix take-51 was tracking) IS an ancestor of PR #1654's branch
   tip, but a later commit on that same branch (`67696aca8`, "Revert...") undid its changes —
   `git merge-base --is-ancestor` proves ancestry, not presence. Confirmed by direct file read:
   `writeUatEnvFile` on PR #1654's branch has no `chatScript` field.
2. The *substance* of that fix (defects 1-3 of #1659) was independently rebuilt and already
   **merged to `origin/main`** via **PR #1660** (merge commit `58e21985e`, merged
   2026-08-17T15:32:26Z). Take-51 checked for the literal hash `52e15e085` on main, correctly found
   it absent, but missed that #1660 landed the same fix under a different commit.
3. The existing worktree `.claude/worktrees/fix-1659-uat-chatscript` is already based on current
   `main` and already has PR #1660's fix (`git merge-base --is-ancestor 58e21985e HEAD` succeeds
   there; confirmed live in `tests/uat/provisioner.ts` — `writeUatEnvFile(...)` is called with
   `chatScript: opts?.chatScript`).

**So there is no branch-base conflict left to resolve.** #1659's original three asks (defects 1-3)
are DONE on main. #1659 stays open only because of **defect 4**, flagged in its own comment thread
as needing a design call, not yet started:

- **Defect 4:** the CLI-tools installer's boot-time drift-reconcile (`install-service.ts` →
  `reconcileInstalledProviders` → `installProvider`/`ensureBinSymlink`) atomically overwrites
  `${JARVIS_CLI_TOOLS_PREFIX}/bin/claude` — the same path the UAT scripted-provider fixture
  (`tests/uat/fixtures/scripted-provider/claude-main.ts`) is installed to — with the real Claude
  CLI on every UAT boot. Result: **no scripted UAT chat turn has ever actually exercised the
  tool-call path**, regardless of the env-var fix. Two directions proposed in #1659:
  - (a) gate the boot reconcile behind an explicit UAT-only flag — touches production
    `install-service.ts` reconcile logic (even if inert outside UAT).
  - (b) give the scripted provider its own PATH entry the installer doesn't own — test-harness-only,
    no production code touched, closer to #1660's precedent (routine tier).
  **Take-52 recommendation: direction (b).** Smaller blast radius, no production reconcile-logic
  change to review, consistent with the existing routine-tier precedent. Not yet executed.
